Method: Introduce selective attention state mechanism and anchor-guided state selection planner; map CP partitions to hierarchical topology to access high-contribution partitions; runtime orchestrates state-centric operations; reuse historical results across denoising steps to reduce communication.

Result: Empirical results show DiTango achieves up to 1.9x end-to-end speedup and 3.2x attention speedup with near-linear scaling in multi-node settings; generation quality comparable to state-of-the-art.

Conclusion: Selective attention and state reuse via an anchor-guided planner can substantially improve diffusion transformer efficiency without sacrificing quality, enabling scalable DiT generation on large clusters.

Abstract: Recent advances in AI-generated content have driven widespread adoption of Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) for high-resolution, long-duration content generation. While parallelization techniques accelerate diffusion inference, they face significant scalability challenges due to excessive communication overhead in multi-node environments.
  We observe that sequence partitions in Context Parallelism (CP) exhibit distinct heterogeneity: spatially proximate partitions contribute more significantly to attention computation results. By mapping this heterogeneous pattern to hierarchical communication topology, we can access high-contribution partitions with reduced communication cost. This insight motivates our novel selective attention state mechanism that strategically balances partial attention computation and historical result reuse across denoising steps.
  We present DiTango, an efficient parallel framework for DiT generation. DiTango features an anchor-guided state selection planner that optimizes computation-reuse decisions for each partition, complemented by a runtime that orchestrates efficient state-centric operations. This design achieves superior system efficiency while preserving generation quality.
  Experimental evaluation on popular diffusion models demonstrates that DiTango achieves up to 1.9x end-to-end and 3.2x attention speedup with near-linear scaling in multi-node settings, while maintaining generation quality comparable to state-of-the-art approaches.

</details>


### [27] [CSS-BA: Gate-Guided Column Space Search for Bundle Adjustment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15652)
*Ayano Kaneda,Takafumi Taketomi,Shugo Yamaguchi,Shigeo Morishima*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Gate-Guided CSS-BA is a solver-side refinement for bundle adjustment that stabilizes Schur-LM in low-parallax/near-rotational settings by constraining update directions to a geometrically informed low-dimensional subspace, without altering the optimization variables or objective; it acts as a drop-in replacement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bundle adjustment often becomes ill-conditioned in weak-geometry regimes (low parallax, near-rotation), causing unreliable pose and calibration estimates. A stable, parameter-preserving solver modification is needed to improve reliability without changing the estimation problem.

Method: Introduce Gate-Guided CSS-BA: a solver-side modification of Schur-LM that preserves BA objective and trust-region framework while restricting each update to a geometrically informed low-dimensional subspace. It combines Column Space Search (CSS) with geometry-aware gating to stabilize updates without removing cameras/points from the optimization; only the update direction is constrained. Serves as a drop-in replacement for existing BA pipelines.

Result: Empirical evaluation on generic and challenging weak-geometry scenarios shows more stable optimization, improved relative pose accuracy, and competitive calibration behavior while preserving reprojection quality.

Conclusion: The approach stabilizes Schur-LM in difficult regimes without altering the underlying estimation problem, offering a practical, drop-in enhancement for BA pipelines.

Abstract: Bundle adjustment (BA) remains a critical refinement module for image-based 3D reconstruction and continues to improve geometric accuracy even in learning-based pipelines. However, in low-parallax and near-rotational regimes, classical Schur-based Levenberg--Marquardt (LM) often becomes ill-conditioned and yields unreliable pose and calibration estimates. We propose Gate-Guided CSS-BA, a solver-side modification of Schur-LM that preserves the classical BA objective and trust-region framework while constraining each update to a geometrically informed low-dimensional subspace. By integrating Column Space Search (CSS) with geometry-aware gating, the method stabilizes the Schur-LM update without altering the estimation problem. In contrast to keyframe or state-selection approaches, all camera and point parameters remain in the optimization problem; only the update direction is restricted. The method serves as a drop-in replacement for existing BA pipelines. Experiments on both generic and challenging weak-geometry scenarios show more stable optimization, improved relative pose accuracy, and competitive calibration behavior while maintaining reprojection quality.

</details>


### [28] [Model Merging for Medical LVLMs: A Benchmark and a Winner-Take-All Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15661)
*Lichao Mou,Shilan Zhang,Chunlei Li,Bingcong Yan,Jingliang Hu,Yilei Shi,Shengwu Xiong,Xiao Xiang Zhu,Lei Li,Yaxiong Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: First medical-domain study of model merging for LVLMs; introduces MergeMedBench and a winner-take-all merging method that outperforms existing approaches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reduce computational and operational overhead of deploying many LoRA-tuned expert LVLMs by merging them into a single model without retraining.

Method: Construct MergeMedBench with eight imaging modalities and diverse clinical task types, comprising 16 LoRA-fine-tuned models built on two mainstream architectures; evaluate existing merging methods; propose winner-take-all (WT A) which keeps only the dominant parameters across experts.

Result: WT A consistently outperforms averaging- or alignment-based merging strategies, is hyperparameter-free, preserves critical parameters, and avoids information dilution; establishes a strong practical baseline for medical LVLM merging.

Conclusion: Presents the first comprehensive study of LoRA merging in medical LVLMs; provides MergeMedBench and a simple, effective merging method, enabling efficient deployment and spurring further research.

Abstract: Large vision-language models (LVLMs) can be adapted to specialized medical imaging tasks via parameter-efficient fine-tuning approaches such as low-rank adaptation (LoRA), leading to a growing ecosystem of expert models tailored to specific imaging modalities and clinical scenarios. However, deploying multiple expert LVLMs in practice incurs substantial computational and operational overhead. Model merging provides a promising solution by consolidating multiple experts into a single model without retraining, yet it remains largely unexplored in the medical domain. In this work, we present the first systematic study of model merging for medical LVLMs. We introduce MergeMedBench, a comprehensive benchmark spanning eight imaging modalities and diverse clinical task types, comprising 16 LoRA fine-tuned models built upon two mainstream architectures. We conduct an extensive evaluation of existing merging methods and further propose winner-take-all, a simple and hyperparameter-free approach that retains only the most dominant parameters across expert models. By preserving the critical parameters that govern model behavior and discarding weaker ones, our method avoids the information dilution inherent in averaging- or alignment-based strategies. Despite its simplicity, winner-take-all consistently outperforms existing approaches, offering both a new perspective on LoRA merging and a strong practical baseline for future research.

</details>


### [29] [PE-Field 4D: Video Generation Models as Canvas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15667)
*Yunpeng Bai,Haoxiang Li,Qixing Huang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A geometry-aware cross-attention mechanism for video diffusion transformers that uses projected positional encoding to align reference content with target views and depth-aware context tokens, enabling controllable video generation under viewpoint changes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Video diffusion models struggle to maintain consistent geometry under camera motion; explicit geometric guidance is needed without disrupting the model's latent structure.

Method: Introduce geometry-aware cross-attention. Encode reference tokens by their projected 2D coordinates in the target view. Provide context tokens with a projected positional encoding that combines target-view reprojection and depth-based disambiguation, while preserving the original spatiotemporal encoding of the video latent. Enable target latent tokens to attend to these structured context tokens, maintaining consistency with the model's native latent structure.

Result: Demonstrates improved spatial controllability in viewpoint-dependent editing tasks (camera re-trajectory, novel-view video synthesis, geometry-aware editing) while preserving the generative priors of the video diffusion model; code released.

Conclusion: Geometry-aware cross-attention with projected positional encoding offers a simple and effective way to inject geometric guidance into video diffusion models, balancing controllability with fidelity.

Abstract: Diffusion Transformers have recently achieved strong performance in video generation, yet controlling scene geometry under viewpoint changes and camera motion remains challenging. In this work, we revisit the role of positional encoding in video diffusion transformers and show that it provides a useful spatial bias for geometry-aware control. Specifically, if reference tokens are encoded according to their projected locations in the target view, the denoising model is encouraged to retrieve content from position aligned regions of the input video. Building on this observation, we introduce a geometry-aware cross-attention mechanism that enables target video latent tokens to attend to structured context tokens derived from reference images or frames. To establish correspondence between the reference content and the target camera trajectory, we equip the context tokens with a projected positional encoding scheme that combines target-view 2D reprojection with depth-aware disambiguation. At the same time, we preserve the original spatiotemporal positional encoding of the generated video latent, allowing geometric guidance to be injected while maintaining consistency with the video model's native latent structure. The resulting framework provides a simple and effective approach for controllable video generation. It improves spatial controllability in viewpoint-dependent editing tasks, including camera re-trajectory, novel-view video synthesis, and geometry-aware video editing, while preserving the generative prior of the underlying video diffusion model. The code is available at: https://github.com/MTLab/PE-Field.

</details>


### [30] [Efficient Frame Selection for Long Videos at Test Time with Attention-Based MLLM Selectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15689)
*Yilin Wang,Xiangxi Zheng,Dongxing Mao,Linjie Li,Zhengyuan Yang,Ping Yu,Rui Yan,Yuan Yao,Alex Jinpeng Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A training-free, budget-aware frame selector (DAFS) leverages cross-modal attention from validation-time MLLMs to convert attention into per-frame relevance scores, enabling efficient frame selection without autoregressive decoding. It jointly allocates candidate pool size and per-frame token budget via dynamic programming.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To break the circular dependency in long-video understanding where selecting informative frames seems to require video understanding, and understanding requires the frames. The approach exploits existing cross-modal attention signals at extraction layers to obtain query-relevant evidence without training.

Method: Use cross-modal attention from validation-selected extraction layers to derive frame-level evidence. Convert selected-layer attention into frame relevance scores via query-conditioned aggregation. Solve joint allocation of candidate pool size and per-frame token budget as a discrete optimization problem, using dynamic programming. Demonstrated with a 32-frame budget; a lightweight 2B-parameter selector generalizes across backbones and tasks with no retraining.

Result: Under a 32-frame budget, DAFS improves over uniform sampling by up to 6.4 points on Video-MME and surpasses prior training-based selectors under matched budgets. It generalizes across selector/answerer backbones and tasks without retraining.

Conclusion: DAFS provides a simple, training-free mechanism to select informative frames by repurposing cross-modal attention, addressing data efficiency and circularity in long-video understanding and enabling effective cross-frame analysis without autoregressive generation.

Abstract: Understanding long videos with multimodal large language models (MLLMs) requires selecting a compact set of frames from thousands of candidates, yet identifying the right frames seemingly requires understanding the video first. We resolve this circular dependency with a simple observation: cross-modal attention at validation-selected extraction layers in MLLMs already provides query-relevant frame evidence without requiring autoregressive generation. We exploit this property to build DAFS (Dynamic Attention-based Budget-aware Frame Selection), a training-free frame selector. A lightweight MLLM selector, even with only 2B parameters, can extract frame-level evidence by converting selected-layer attention into relevance scores through query-conditioned aggregation. This enables cross-frame comparison without autoregressive decoding. To handle the selector's own context constraint, we formulate the joint allocation of candidate pool size and per-frame token budget as a discrete optimization problem solved by dynamic programming. Under a 32-frame budget, our selector improves over uniform sampling by up to 6.4 points on Video-MME and outperforms prior training-based selectors under matched frame budgets, while generalizing across selector and answerer backbones, and across tasks, without retraining.

</details>


### [31] [Hierarchical Specialised Ensembles for Classification of Zebrafish Phenotypes Using the Selected Image Recognition Methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15698)
*Piotr S. Maciąg,Monika Maciąg,Magdalena Majdan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes and evaluates three hierarchical ensemble setups for zebrafish embryo phenotype classification, comparing backbones and concluding that ConvNeXt performs best overall while a specialized hierarchical ensemble (setup 2) offers the best F1-score balance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve zebrafish phenotype recognition from embryo images by evaluating hierarchical ensemble designs and backbone architectures, and to identify a configuration that balances accuracy across phenotypes.

Method: Stage 1 uses a four-class classifier to label images as Normal, Chorion, Dead, or Other. Stage 2 varies by setup: (1) a single multi-label classifier; (2) two specialized multi-label classifiers; (3) an ensemble of binary classifiers. Experiments compare ResNet18, ViT, and ConvNeXt as backbones.

Result: ConvNeXt backbone yields best performance across setups; the specialized hierarchical ensemble in setup 2 achieves the best balance in F1-score across phenotypes.

Conclusion: Specialized hierarchical ensembles are effective for zebrafish phenotype recognition, and ConvNeXt is a particularly useful backbone for this task.

Abstract: We propose and evaluate three hierarchical ensemble setups for zebrafish phenotype classification from embryo images. In all setups, stage 1 uses a single four-class classifier to assign images to one of the exclusive phenotypes: Normal, Chorion, Dead, or Other. Images classified as Other are then processed in stage 2, where the ensemble design differs across setups: a single multi-label classifier, two specialized multi-label classifiers, or an ensemble of binary classifiers. We compare these setups using three backbone architectures: ResNet18, ViT, and ConvNeXt. Overall, ConvNeXt achieves the best performance across setups, while the specialized hierarchical ensemble in setup 2 provides the best balance in terms of F1-score. The results show that the proposed specialised hierarchical ensembles are effective for zebrafish phenotype recognition, and suggest that ConvNeXt is particularly useful backbone model.

</details>


### [32] [GoStop: Reinforcement Learning for Adaptive Temporal Aggregation in Event-Based Feature Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15699)
*Youngho Kim,Hoonhee Cho,Jae-Young Kang,Kuk-Jin Yoon*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: An RL-based adaptive control of event accumulation for online feature tracking with event cameras; introduces a Dynamic Event-based Tracking (DEFT) dataset; claims robustness under dynamic motion and improved accuracy-efficiency trade-offs by replacing hand-crafted accumulation heuristics with a learnable policy; plug-and-play integration with existing trackers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fixed heuristic event accumulation rules in event-based feature tracking fail under varying and abrupt motion; need a data-driven, adaptive mechanism to balance tracking accuracy and computational efficiency.

Method: Model event accumulation as a sequential decision-making problem and train a reinforcement learning agent to decide whether to continue accumulating events or to perform tracking inference. The agent's decision depends on motion cues. Introduce a Dynamic Temporal agent for adaptive accumulation and a DEFT dataset to evaluate robustness under dynamic motion distributions. The proposed framework is designed as a plug-and-play component compatible with existing trackers.

Result: Empirical results show that the RL-based adaptive accumulation improves robustness to dynamic motion and achieves a favorable balance between tracking accuracy and efficiency compared to heuristic-based methods. The DEFT dataset enables evaluation under dynamic motion distributions. Code and datasets are released at the provided URL.

Conclusion: Adaptive RL-driven control of event accumulation offers robustness and efficiency gains for online event-based feature tracking and can be integrated as a plug-in with existing methods. The DEFT dataset provides a benchmark for dynamic motion scenarios and supports reproducibility.

Abstract: Feature tracking plays a fundamental role in understanding scene motion and supports various downstream tasks. Event cameras, with their high temporal resolution and asynchronous sensing, enable low-latency and motion-robust perception, making them well-suited for feature tracking under fast and non-linear motion. However, existing event-based feature tracking methods rely on fixed heuristic rules based on hand-tuning for event accumulation. Such strategies fail to adapt to diverse motion dynamics, leading to degraded performance under abrupt motion changes or low-motion scenarios. In this paper, we model event accumulation as a sequential decision-making problem and introduce reinforcement learning (RL) framework to adaptively control the accumulation process for online event-based feature tracking. Our approach trains a RL agent that decides whether to continue accumulating events or to perform tracking inference based on motion cues. The proposed adaptive temporal agent enables dynamic adaptation to varying motion patterns without relying on hand-crafted rules. Furthermore, we introduce a Dynamic Event-based Tracking (DEFT) dataset with dynamic motion distributions to evaluate the robustness of the feature tracking. Extensive experiments demonstrate that integrating our plug-and-play framework to existing feature tracking methods consistently outperforms heuristic-based approaches, improving robustness under dynamic motion while offering a better balance between tracking accuracy and efficiency. Our project codes and datasets are available at https://github.com/kmax2001/GoSTOP

</details>


### [33] [Efficient Difficulty-Aware Dynamic Routing for Diffusion-Based Real-World Image Super-Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15711)
*Xue Wu,Kang Zhao,Kafeng Wang,Jianfei Chen,Jingwei Xin,Nannan Wang,Xinbo Gao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes Difficulty-aware Dynamic Routing (DDR) for Real-ISR, routing images to SD-based networks with varying VAE downsampling to match restoration difficulty, improving efficiency and quality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current SD-based Real-ISR approaches use a uniform processing pipeline and aggressive VAE downsampling, causing inefficiency and irreversible loss of fine details. There is a need for per-image adaptive capacity and better preservation of high-frequency information.

Method: Train a difficulty estimator to predict the restoration cost of each input and assign it to one of multiple Real-ISR networks with different capacities. Vary the spatial downsampling ratio of the VAE in the diffusion backbone to create networks with different information preservation levels, enabling higher capacity for harder cases while keeping efficiency for easier ones.

Result: Empirical results show that DDR achieves superior efficiency and restoration performance compared to recent state-of-the-art Real-ISR methods on benchmark tests.

Conclusion: DDR introduces a dynamic, difficulty-aware routing scheme that aligns network capacity with restoration difficulty and mitigates irreversible detail loss from aggressive downsampling, yielding better trade-offs between quality and efficiency.

Abstract: Diffusion-based methods have achieved impressive performance in real-world image super-resolution (Real-ISR) by leveraging large pre-trained stable diffusion (SD) models as powerful generative priors. However, these methods still face two key limitations. First, existing SD-based one-step and multi-step Real-ISR approaches adopt a unified processing paradigm for all input samples, ignoring the varying restoration difficulty across images. Second, the aggressive resolution reduction of the VAE in SD models (e.g., 8x downsampling) leads to irreversible loss of fine-scale details, which cannot be recovered by the subsequent diffusion process.
  To address these limitations, we propose a Difficulty-aware Dynamic Routing (DDR) strategy that overcomes the rigid, one-size-fits-all processing paradigm. Specifically, we first design a difficulty estimator to predict the restoration cost of each input image, enabling automatic assignment to a network of appropriate capacity. Then, we construct a set of Real-ISR networks with varying model capacities by modulating the spatial downsampling ratio of the VAE in the SD backbone, thereby preserving more high-frequency information for challenging cases while maintaining efficiency for simpler inputs.
  Extensive experiments have demonstrated the superior efficiency and effectiveness of the proposed model compared to recent state-of-the-art methods.

</details>


### [34] [Per-Stroke Temporal Control for Text-to-Motion via Action Units and Action-Detection Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15717)
*Euijun Jung,Youngki Lee*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces Action Units (AUs) as typed temporal events to explicitly condition per-stroke motion in text-to-motion systems, using a gated adapter and a training-free inference-time gradient to correct timing, yielding superior per-stroke placement on StrokeBench compared to baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Text-to-motion models adeptly produce the intended action name but struggle with temporal alignment; existing interfaces (text, interval, or frame-level) fail to reliably place individual strokes in time. AU provides explicit, controllable temporal events to anchor each stroke.

Method: Define typed temporal events (AUs) encoding stroke body track, action class, time window, and impact timing. Ground a frozen text-to-motion backbone with a lightweight gated adapter that injects per-stroke tokens and a per-frame phase channel. Use a training-free classifier gradient from a frozen frame-level detector at inference to correct residual timing. Evaluate on StrokeBench with stimuli specifying count, ordering, track, core-frame placement, and paired with an audited stroke corpus.

Result: AU grounding significantly increases the correct placement rate of individual strokes, surpassing the strongest prior interface, while achieving the best motion quality among text-, interval-, and frame-level baselines. The core-frame prompt adds a further axis of steerability.

Conclusion: Explicit temporal grounding via Action Units improves per-stroke temporal control in text-to-motion generation, with the anchored classifier gradient providing timing corrections; the approach enables more precise sequencing of strokes and offers an additional steerable axis through the core-frame prompt.

Abstract: Text-to-motion models are competent at the action a prompt names but unreliable at when each stroke lands: four punches alternating left and right rarely return four separable strokes. We introduce typed temporal events called Action Units (AUs) that make the individual stroke -- its body track, action class, time window, and impact timing -- an explicit conditioning signal. We ground a frozen text-to-motion backbone on the AU set through a lightweight gated adapter injecting two streams (per-stroke tokens and a per-frame phase channel), and at inference close residual timing errors with a training-free classifier gradient from a frozen frame-level detector. We measure per-stroke control on StrokeBench, whose prompts specify count, ordering, track, and core-frame placement, paired with an audited stroke corpus. AU grounding markedly raises the rate of correctly placed single strokes over the strongest prior interface, at the best motion quality among text-, interval-, and frame-level baselines. The prompted core frame emerges as a further steerable axis.

</details>


### [35] [Event3R: Asynchronous-to-Global 3D Reconstruction from Event Camera via Spatial-Temporal Feature Aggregation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15727)
*Jian Huang,Haotian Shen,Xinhao Lou,Chengrui Dong,Wenpu Li,Peidong Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Event3R introduces a feed-forward method to convert asynchronous event streams into globally consistent 3D point clouds. It uses spatial-temporal voxels and a temporal attention module, with Masked Bin Modeling (MBM) for self-supervised pre-training and contrastive alignment/consistency losses during fine-tuning, achieving robust, temporally coherent reconstructions that outperform prior event-based methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bridge the gap between dense 3D reconstruction successes on frame-based data (e.g., DUSt3R) and the asynchronous, sparse, highly dynamic nature of event cameras, compounded by limited labeled data.

Method: Represent events as spatial-temporal voxels to enable time-aware feature integration via a temporal attention module. Employ Masked Bin Modeling (MBM) for self-supervised pre-training to learn robust temporal representations with minimal labeled data, and fine-tune with contrastive alignment and consistency regularization losses across views to enforce structural and temporal coherence.

Result: Extensive experiments on synthetic and real-world benchmarks show robust, temporally consistent, and globally aligned 3D reconstructions, with significant performance gains over existing event-based methods.

Conclusion: Event3R demonstrates effective temporal-aware representation learning for event-based 3D reconstruction, achieving strong generalization and data efficiency through MBM pre-training and cross-view regularization, advancing practical robotic perception from event data.

Abstract: Robust 3D reconstruction is essential for robotics and embodied perception. Recent feed-forward approaches such as DUSt3R have demonstrated impressive progress in dense 3D reconstruction from RGB images, achieving global geometric consistency and strong generalization. However, extending such dense 3D reconstruction to event cameras remains challenging due to their asynchronous, sparse, and highly dynamic nature, as well as the lack of large-scale, well-labeled datasets. In this work, we introduce Event3R, a feed-forward framework that directly maps asynchronous event streams to globally consistent 3D point clouds. Event3R represents incoming events as spatial-temporal voxels, enabling time-aware feature integration through a temporal attention module that enhances the module's temporal feature learning. To further strengthen temporal representation learning and reduce reliance on labeled data, we propose a Masked Bin Modeling (MBM) strategy for self-supervised pre-training, enabling robust temporal representation learning with minimal labeled data, and retain it as an auxiliary fine-tuning objective. In addition, contrastive alignment and consistency regularization losses are incorporated during fine-tuning to reinforce structural correspondence and temporal coherence across views. Extensive experiments on both synthetic and real-world benchmarks demonstrate that Event3R achieves robust, temporally consistent, and globally aligned 3D reconstructions, significantly outperforming existing event-based methods.

</details>


### [36] [IoUPD: IoU-Aware Privileged Distillation for Visual Grounding with Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15732)
*Xiuyuan Zhu,Ke Lu,Hao Wu,Zijin Du,Dongming Zhang,Jian Xue*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: IoUPD introduces IoU-aware privileged distillation for coordinate-generating multimodal LLMs in visual grounding; training uses privileged guidance from ground-truth boxes, enabling region-level improvements without extra inference-time modules.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Mismatch between training on token-level coordinate strings and evaluation based on geometric overlap; need to align optimization objective with grounding quality.

Method: A student-teacher setup where a frozen teacher with box-marked image and augmented prompt provides privileged guidance during training; privileged distillation loss weights tokens by geometric importance and teacher reliability; standard supervised fine-tuning anchor used.

Result: Empirical gains on standard referring-expression grounding benchmarks, with consistent region-level improvements over strong coordinate-generating baselines; shows boxes offer useful privileged guidance beyond coordinate labels.

Conclusion: IoUPD achieves improvements without box overlays or extra inference-time components, suggesting privileged guidance from ground-truth boxes can enhance coordinate generation.

Abstract: Visual grounding with multimodal large language models is commonly formulated as autoregressive coordinate generation, where a model outputs bounding-box coordinates as text given an image and a referring-expression prompt. While this interface is simple and compatible with instruction following, it introduces a mismatch between training and evaluation: training optimizes token-level likelihood over coordinate strings, whereas grounding quality is measured by geometric overlap. We propose IoUPD, an IoU-aware privileged distillation method for coordinate-generating multimodal large language models. IoUPD uses ground-truth boxes not only as coordinate targets, but also as privileged training-time guidance. During training, the student receives the original image and prompt, while a frozen teacher receives a box-marked image and an augmented prompt that indicates the marked region. The student is trained with a supervised fine-tuning anchor and a privileged distillation loss whose token weights reflect both geometric importance and teacher reliability. At inference time, IoUPD requires no box overlay, privileged hint, teacher branch, or additional prediction module. Experiments on standard referring-expression grounding benchmarks show consistent region-level improvements over strong coordinate-generating baselines, demonstrating that ground-truth boxes can provide useful privileged guidance beyond serving as coordinate labels.

</details>


### [37] [The Third Competition on Document Forgery Detection on ID-Cards and Passports](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15734)
*Juan E. Tapia,Mario Nieto,Juan M. Espin,Álvaro S. Rocamora,Javier Barrachina,Naser Damer,Christoph Busch*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Incode dominates the Third International Competition on Document Forgery Detection for ID cards and passports across two tracks, demonstrating that robust PAD requires generalizability and consistency across diverse conditions, and establishing a leading, reproducible benchmark with broad community participation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Benchmark and advance presentation attack detection (PAD) for ID documents under synthetic and heterogeneous real-world-like attacks; promote collaboration between industry and academia and provide a reproducible standard for performance evaluation.

Method: Two competition tracks: Track 1 uses synthetic data-based PAD under controlled but diverse conditions; Track 2 introduces heterogeneous, cross-domain attack scenarios. Performance evaluated with AV_Rank across thresholds. The study also reports large-scale participation (63+ teams, >100 models).

Result: Incode achieved top AV_Rank in both tracks (27.82% in Track 1; 68.71% in Track 2), outperforming some baselines and established methods. The results emphasize that PAD effectiveness depends on consistency across attack types and imaging conditions, and underscore the competition’s role as a leading benchmark for PAD on ID documents.

Conclusion: The competition demonstrates that effective PAD requires balanced, generalizable designs that perform well across varied attacks and imaging scenarios. The public-private collaboration and large-scale evaluations enhance reproducibility and real-world applicability, solidifying the event as a standard benchmark for secure identity verification.

Abstract: This paper presents a comprehensive analysis of the results from the Third International Competition on Document Forgery Detection on ID-Cards and Passports, which was held across two distinct tracks. Track 1 evaluates a synthetic-data-based ID-PAD system under controlled but diverse conditions, where the winning team, \textit{Incode}, achieves an $AV_{Rank}$ of 27.82%, confirming consistent performance across metrics and highlighting the importance of a balanced, generalizable design. In Track 2, the challenge intensifies with heterogeneous attack scenarios across different domains, where \textit{Incode} again achieved the top position with an $AV_{Rank}$ of 68.71% across thresholds, outperforming some baselines and established methods. These results demonstrate that PAD effectiveness requires not only high accuracy but also consistency across diverse attack types and imaging conditions. The success of this initiative across both tracks underscores the value of collaboration between companies and academic teams. This year, more than \textit{63 teams} were registered, and more than \textit{100 submission models} were evaluated. This competition has evolved into a leading benchmark state-of-the-art in PAD on ID documents, setting the standard for performance, reproducibility, and real-world applicability in secure identity verification.

</details>


### [38] [Debiasing Text-to-Image Evaluation via Implicit Cultural Alignment Reward Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15740)
*Bo-An Chang,Yu-Chih Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes an Implicit Cultural Alignment Reward Model (ICARM) using a 4.2B Multimodal LLM with Skip-connection Cross-Attention to preserve culturally salient cues, enabling fast, non-autoregressive evaluation for T2I content. Outperforms baseline metrics on CulturalFrames with strong speedups, suitable as a scalar signal for RLHF/DPO.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current T2I evaluation relies on visual-semantic representations and autoregressive VQA, which underrepresent implicit cultural norms and hinder real-time, scalable reward modeling.

Method: Introduce ICARM built on a lightweight 4.2B MLLM. Integrate an Implicit Cultural Probe with a SkipCA mechanism so late-stage semantic features can attend early-stage visual representations, preserving culturally salient details.

Result: Evaluated on 3,323 image pairs from CulturalFrames, achieving 80.54% pairwise accuracy, Pearson 0.546, Kendall 0.377, outperforming representative vision-language metrics and MLLMs. Non-autoregressive evaluation runs in 0.21s per item, offering ~10x speedup over VQA-based evaluators.

Conclusion: ICARM provides an efficient, culturally aware scalar signal for preference optimization pipelines (e.g., RLHF, DPO) in T2I evaluation, enabling scalable real-time feedback while better capturing implicit cultural norms.

Abstract: As Text-to-Image (T2I) systems rapidly advance, evaluating the cultural authenticity of synthesized content has become increasingly important for fair and trustworthy generative AI. Existing T2I evaluation metrics and multimodal judges often rely on visual-semantic representations that underrepresent implicit cultural norms, leading to biased preference judgments and the omission of fine-grained cultural cues. In addition, visual question answering (VQA)-based evaluators typically depend on autoregressive text generation, which limits their scalability for real-time reward modeling. To address these limitations, we introduce an Implicit Cultural Alignment Reward Model built upon a lightweight 4.2-billion-parameter Multimodal Large Language Model (MLLM). Our framework integrates an Implicit Cultural Probe with a Skip-connection Cross-Attention (SkipCA) mechanism, enabling late-stage semantic features to directly attend to early-stage visual representations and better preserve culturally salient details. Evaluations on 3,323 challenging and carefully curated image pairs from the CulturalFrames benchmark show that our approach achieves 80.54% pairwise accuracy, with Pearson and Kendall correlation coefficients of 0.546 and 0.377, respectively, outperforming representative vision-language metrics and MLLM-based evaluators. Moreover, by bypassing autoregressive text generation, our model processes each evaluation in 0.21 seconds under our local inference setup, achieving a $10\times$ speedup over standard VQA-based evaluators. These results suggest that the proposed reward model can provide an efficient and culturally aware scalar signal for preference optimization pipelines such as Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback and Direct Preference Optimization.

</details>


### [39] [EgoExoMoCap: Distributed Ego-Exo Human Motion Capture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15868)
*Jiaxi Jiang,Bharat Lal Bhatnagar,Nan Yang,Lingni Ma,Sebastian Starke,Robin Kips,Nadine Bertsch,Christian Holz,Federica Bogo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Distributed ego-exo motion capture for HMDs using smart glasses, fusing ego- and exocentric signals with DINOv3 features to recover 3D motion without bulky setups.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing MoCap approaches rely on either egocentric (wearer-centric) or exocentric (surroundings-centric) tracking. There is a need for scalable, real-world motion capture from minimal gear by combining both paradigms to improve robustness and coverage, especially under noise and occlusions.

Method: A distributed framework (EgoExoMoCap) with multiple wearers, each outfitted with smart glasses. It uses head tracking (and possibly wrist signals) to estimate global 3D motion and leverages context-aware image features based on DINOv3 to enhance robustness by fusing ego- and exocentric signals across modalities.

Result: Experiments on two in-the-wild datasets show robust motion reconstruction even in challenging scenarios, indicating effective fusion of ego- and exocentric cues with learned image features.

Conclusion: EgoExoMoCap provides a simple, scalable, headset-based MoCap solution that avoids bulky setups by jointly exploiting ego- and exocentric signals and strong image representations, validated in real-world data.

Abstract: Human motion capture from head-mounted devices (HMDs) offers a scalable way to acquire real-world human motion and interaction data, which is crucial for applications in embodied AI and VR/AR. Existing approaches focus on either egocentric body tracking, estimating the motion of the subject wearing the device, or exocentric tracking, capturing the movements of people in the wearer's surroundings. So far, these two paradigms have largely been explored in isolation. In this paper, we propose a novel distributed framework that jointly leverages ego- and exocentric multi-modal signals for human motion estimation from HMDs. Unlike traditional motion capture systems requiring bulky multi-camera setups or obtrusive mocap suits, our approach, EgoExoMoCap, is as simple as two (or more) people, each wearing a pair of smart glasses. The method leverages head (plus potentially wrist) tracking signals for accurate estimation of global motion in the 3D world and combines context-aware image features based on DINOv3 to achieve robustness in the presence of noise and occlusions. Extensive experiments on two in-the-wild datasets show that our approach can robustly reconstruct motion even in challenging scenarios.

</details>


### [40] [Personalized Image Aesthetic Assessment via Preference-rich Sample Mining and Cohort Merging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15752)
*Zhichao Yang,Tianjiao Gu,Zhixianhe Zhang,Xiangfei Sheng,Pengfei Chen,Leida Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PRAC: A Multimodal LLM-based approach for personalized image aesthetic assessment that uses Preference-Rich sample mining and Aesthetically-resonant Cohort merging to model individual preferences, achieving state-of-the-art results on four PIAA datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Aesthetic preferences vary across individuals and stimuli; limited user data hinders personalization. Leveraging cross-user similarities and cohort-level patterns can improve personalization and generalization in PIAA.

Method: PRAC identifies preference-rich samples via Collective Controversy and Personalized Deviation, computes cross-user preference similarities through preference embeddings, and merges models by aggregating aesthetically-resonant users to personalize for a target individual, all within an MLLM-based framework.

Result: Empirical evaluation on four benchmark PIAA databases shows PRAC outperforms state-of-the-art methods. Code and model will be released at https://github.com/yzc-ippl/PRAC.

Conclusion: PRAC effectively captures individual aesthetic preferences and enhances personalization through targeted sample mining and cohort-based model merging, demonstrating strong performance and generalization across datasets.

Abstract: Personalized Image Aesthetic Assessment (PIAA) aims to predict aesthetic ratings of images that vary across individuals. The aesthetic preferences manifest to different extents across distinct visual stimuli and exhibit cohort-specific patterns. Motivated by the above fact, this paper presents a Multimodal Large Language Model (MLLM)-based approach, which models individual aesthetic preferences by Preference-Rich sample mining and Aesthetically-resonant Cohort merging (PRAC). Specifically, PRAC first identifies preference-rich samples by analyzing both Collective Controversy and Personalized Deviation of images, maximizing the utility of limited user data. Based upon the preference-rich samples, cross-user preference similarities are measured by comparing preference embeddings. Then, a cohort-based model merging strategy, is proposed by aggregating preference patterns from aesthetically-resonant users, which further enhances the personalization for the target individual. Extensive experiments and comparisons on four benchmark PIAA databases demonstrate the superiority of the proposed PRAC model over the state-of-the-arts. The code and model will be public at https://github.com/yzc-ippl/PRAC.

</details>


### [41] [Orbis 2: A Hierarchical World Model for Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15898)
*Sudhanshu Mittal,Arian Mousakhan,Silvio Galesso,Karim Farid,Jonannes Dienert,Rajat Sahay,Thomas Brox*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Hierarchical driving world model with a high-level predictor for long-horizon structure and a low-level generator for detailed predictions, trained in two stages (diffusion forcing pretraining then teacher forcing fine-tuning) to achieve state-of-the-art results on driving benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current world models are typically single-level, prioritizing perceptual fidelity but lacking multi-scale spatial-semantic reasoning necessary for real-world planning and long-horizon prediction.

Method: A two-level hierarchy: a high-level predictor forecasting coarse scene structure over extended horizons, and a low-level generator producing detailed predictions conditioned on the high-level output. Pretraining uses a diffusion forcing objective to enrich representations; fine-tuning uses teacher forcing for stable autoregressive rollouts; a two-stage training paradigm combines both.

Result: State-of-the-art performance across driving world-model evaluations, including long-horizon generation fidelity, steering responsiveness in counterfactual scenarios, and internal representation quality.

Conclusion: A hierarchical, two-stage training approach (diffusion forcing pretraining + teacher forcing fine-tuning) yields high perceptual fidelity and robust rollouts, producing superior driving world models.

Abstract: Current world models operate at a single level of abstraction, with most prioritizing perceptual fidelity while lacking the spatial reasoning and semantic understanding required for real-world downstream tasks. We present a hierarchical driving world model that factorizes future prediction across two levels operating at distinct temporal and abstraction scales: a high-level predictor that forecasts coarse scene structure over extended temporal horizons, and a low-level generator that produces detailed predictions conditioned on the high-level output. This decomposition yields high perceptual fidelity while also capturing strong spatial and semantic representations. We further show that pretraining with a diffusion forcing objective yields substantially richer internal representations than the standard teacher forcing objective, while teacher forcing -- predicting only the next frame from clean context -- produces more stable autoregressive rollouts. We therefore introduce a generic two-stage training paradigm that pretrains the model with diffusion forcing and fine-tunes with teacher forcing, combining the representational benefits of the former with the rollout stability of the latter. Our approach achieves state-of-the-art results across the standard suite of driving world model evaluations on established benchmarks, including long-horizon generation fidelity, steering responsiveness evaluated on counterfactual scenarios, and internal representation quality. Project page with code, demo, checkpoints and qualitative results: https://lmb-freiburg.github.io/orbis2.github.io/

</details>


### [42] [GeoChrono: Benchmarking and Rethinking Long-Term Temporal Understanding in Remote Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15768)
*Yujie Li,Jiancheng Pan,Zhiwei Wei,Jiuniu Wang,Mugen Peng,Wenjia Xu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposes ChronoBench, a four-level remote-sensing benchmark with 12 sub-tasks and 17,689 QA pairs to evaluate land-cover evolution reasoning; introduces GeoChrono with TempEnc and C2FComp, plus ChronoInstruct (104K data), achieving SOTA on ChronoBench and notable efficiency gains; identifies long-term memory as key bottleneck.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically quantify and dissect the distinct cognitive competencies required for modeling long-term land-cover evolution, addressing the lack of a unified evaluation framework.

Method: ChronoBench decomposes the task into four progressive levels (Land Cover Perception, Temporal Recognition, Long-Term Memory, Spatio-Temporal Reasoning) across 12 sub-tasks; GeoChrono employs a Temporal Trajectory Encoder (TempEnc) and a Coarse-to-Fine Token Compressor (C2FComp); ChronoInstruct provides 104K instruction-tuning samples; evaluation on ChronoBench against mainstream MLLMs; GitHub resource for code and data.

Result: Mainstream MLLMs lag humans, with Long-Term Memory identified as the most critical bottleneck; GeoChrono achieves state-of-the-art on ChronoBench, outperforming leading commercial MLLMs by over 20%; C2FComp reduces visual tokens by over 56% while maintaining 94.6% of GeoChrono's performance.

Conclusion: The study presents a structured framework to assess and address temporal cognition in remote sensing, highlights long-term memory as a bottleneck, and introduces architectural components (TempEnc, C2FComp) and a large instruction-tuning dataset (ChronoInstruct) to push toward robust long-term land-cover evolution modeling.

Abstract: Remote sensing offers an unparalleled vantage point for observing the Earth's long-term surface evolution, yet it demands that a model not only perceive land cover at isolated moments, but also track changes, memorize evolution histories, and reason across time and space. However, existing studies lack a systematic evaluation that dissects these distinct competencies. To fill this gap, we introduce ChronoBench, a multidimensional benchmark that decomposes this task into four progressive cognitive levels (i.e., Land Cover Perception, Temporal Recognition, Long-Term Memory, and Spatio-Temporal Reasoning). The ChronoBench comprises 12 sub-tasks and 17,689 rigorously validated QA (Question-Answer) pairs. Extensive evaluations reveal that mainstream MLLMs fall drastically behind human experts, with Long-Term Memory emerging as the most critical bottleneck. Motivated by this finding, we further propose GeoChrono, an MLLM with enhanced capabilities for tracing, memorizing, and reasoning about long-term geographic evolution. Leveraging the physical prior that geographic parcels remain spatially fixed while their semantics evolve, we design a Temporal Trajectory Encoder~(TempEnc) that constructs per-location temporal trajectories for dedicated land cover evolution modeling, and we introduce a Coarse-to-Fine Token Compressor~(C2FComp) that adaptively preserves dynamic regions while compressing the static background. To support training, we also construct ChronoInstruct, a 104K-sample instruction-tuning dataset spanning all competency levels for training. GeoChrono achieves state-of-the-art performance on ChronoBench, surpassing the leading commercial MLLMs by over 20%, while C2FComp reduces visual tokens by over 56% while retaining GeoChrono's 94.6% performance. The code and data will be available at https://github.com/IntelliSensing/GeoChrono

</details>


### [43] [DPNeXt: A Lightweight Multi-Scale Feature Fusion Framework for Efficient ViT-Based Multi-Task Dense Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16012)
*Jehun Kang,Jungha Wang,Youngjun Hwang,David Hyunchul Shim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DPNeXt is a lightweight, multi-scale feature fusion decoder for VFM-based multi-task perception (semantic segmentation + depth) that improves efficiency and reduces negative transfer via MTBG, achieving state-of-the-art results on Cityscapes and NYUv2 with substantially fewer parameters than DPT.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address bottlenecks in decoding strategies when using Vision Foundation Models for multi-task perception in robotics, focusing on efficiency, effective feature fusion, and mitigating negative inductive transfer between tasks.

Method: Proposes DPNeXt, a streamlined multi-scale feature fusion decoder built with dual depthwise separable inverted bottlenecks, enabling fusion-centric decoding and independent task modularization. Introduces Multi-Task Boundary Guidance (MTBG), symmetric boundary-focused supervision that promotes geometric consistency without extra annotations or inference cost, operating on frozen VFMs with a modular MTL design.

Result: Cityscapes: DPNeXt-S surpasses prior MTL SOTA; DPNeXt-B yields further gains. NYUv2: DPNeXt-B achieves best semantic segmentation and depth among compared methods, with substantially fewer trainable parameters than prior large-scale MTL models. DPNeXt-S reduces trainable parameters by 78.6% vs standard DPT and has the fastest inference on resource-constrained laptop hardware among the baselines.

Conclusion: DPNeXt delivers an efficient, high-performance MTL decoding solution for VFM-based perception, with MTBG effectively reducing negative transfer and enabling compact models. The approach achieves strong results on Cityscapes and NYUv2 and is suitable for deployment on limited hardware; code and models will be released.

Abstract: Multi-Task Learning (MTL) in robotics perception systems supports comprehensive 3D spatial scene understanding by integrating semantic segmentation and depth estimation. While Vision Foundation Models (VFMs) are increasingly adopted as robust feature encoders, existing decoding strategies present a critical bottleneck. To address this, we propose DPNeXt, a streamlined multi-scale feature fusion decoder and efficient alternative to the standard Dense Prediction Transformer (DPT). DPNeXt uses dual depthwise separable inverted bottlenecks to improve frozen VFM utilization through fusion-centric decoding and independent task modularization. To further mitigate negative inductive transfer between tasks, we introduce the Multi-Task Boundary Guidance (MTBG) strategy. Unlike prior boundary-aware methods that add fusion modules or gating, MTBG applies symmetric boundary-focused supervision to encourage geometric consistency without extra annotation or inference cost. Experiments on Cityscapes show that DPNeXt-S outperforms prior state-of-the-art (SOTA) MTL models, while DPNeXt-B further improves the overall performance and achieves the best results among the compared methods. On NYUv2, DPNeXt-B also achieves the best semantic segmentation and depth estimation results among the compared methods while requiring substantially fewer trainable parameters than prior large-scale MTL models. Compared with the standard DPT, DPNeXt-S reduces trainable parameters by 78.6% and achieves the fastest inference speed among the compared models on resource-constrained laptop hardware. The source code, model checkpoints, and a demo video will be made available at https://github.com/kangjehun/DPNeXt.

</details>


### [44] [SlotMem: Character-Addressable Internal Memory for Narrative Long Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15772)
*Yilai Liu,Xin Zhang,Shiyuan Zhang,Hongyang Du*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces SlotMem, a character-addressable internal memory for long narrative video generation that localizes character-relevant tokens via a Character-Semantic Probe, compresses them into role-wise slots, and uses conservative updates with character-wise cross-attention to improve long-range character consistency while maintaining video quality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Global consistency methods rely on cues that are not aligned with preserving character identity; coarse frame-level memory entangles identity with incidental visuals and lacks a continuous update mechanism under memory constraints.

Method: Character-Semantic Probe localizes character-relevant tokens from cross-attention; Memory Encoder compresses DiT tokens into compact role-wise slot memory; Memory Writer conservatively updates each character's memory with new observations; Character-Wise Cross-Attention retrieves role memory and injects it into localized tokens of the same character.

Result: Experiments on multiple narrative long video generation benchmarks show SlotMem improves long-range character consistency over existing baselines, while maintaining comparable video quality.

Conclusion: SlotMem provides a memory-based framework that decouples character identity from incidental factors, enabling continuous, memory-efficient updates for more consistent multi-character narratives in long videos.

Abstract: Maintaining recurring character identities across scene transitions and long temporal gaps is a central challenge in narrative long video generation. Methods targeting global consistency often retrieve memory using cues that are not aligned with character identity preservation, while recent character-centric variants still rely on coarse frame-level kv memory that entangles identity with incidental visual factors and lacks a continuous update mechanism under limited memory capacity. To address these limitations, we propose \textbf{SlotMem}, a character-addressable internal memory framework for multi-character narrative long video generation. Specifically, SlotMem uses a Character-Semantic Probe to localize character-relevant visual tokens from cross-attention responses, and a Memory Encoder to compress DiT tokens into compact role-wise slot memory. As generation proceeds, a Memory Writer conservatively updates each character's memory with new observations, while Character-Wise Cross-Attention retrieves the role memory and injects it only into localized tokens of the same character. Experiments on multiple narrative long video generation benchmarks show that SlotMem improves long-range character consistency over existing baselines, while maintaining comparable video quality. Our code is available at https://github.com/YilaiLiu-HKU/SlotMem.

</details>


### [45] [PIXIE: A Zero-Shot texture-invariant 6D pose estimation framework for unseen objects with assembly defects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16015)
*Leon Jungemeyer,Alejandro Magaña,Gautham Mohan,Matthias Karl,Daniel Werdehausen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Zero-shot 6D pose estimation from RGB using an untextured 3D model; renders synthetic depth/normal maps, matches via cross-modality features, and uses PnP with filtered correspondences for robust pose; achieves state-of-the-art on texture-less objects and a new dataset with defects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reduce reliance on textured 3D models and image pipelines; enable robust pose estimation in industrial settings with potential object damage or assembly defects; achieve zero-shot deployment without object-specific training.

Method: Render depth and normal maps from sampled viewpoints on the untextured 3D model. Use a pretrained cross-modality feature matcher to align the synthetic maps with the query RGB image, obtain 2D–3D correspondences via back-projection of matched keypoints, and estimate pose with PnP. Apply correspondence filtering to tolerate geometric deviations between model and object and rely on geometry for robustness to lighting/texture.

Result: Demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on texture-less objects without object-specific training on public benchmarks. Introduces a novel dataset featuring assembly defects, texture variation, and occlusion to validate real-world applicability.

Conclusion: A geometry-driven, zero-shot framework (PIXIE) can robustly estimate 6D pose from RGB for texture-less objects, maintaining accuracy under lighting/texture changes and object deviations, suitable for industrial deployment.

Abstract: 6D pose estimation remains a key challenge in robotics and computer vision, particularly in industrial environments. The deployment of currently available data-driven methods is often limited by resource-intensive data pipelines, reliance on textured 3D models, and sensitivity to geometric deviations caused by damages or assembly defects. We present PIXIE, a zero-shot framework that estimates the 6D pose of an object from an RGB image using only an untextured 3D model. Synthetic depth and normal maps are rendered from sampled reference viewpoints and matched to the query image via a pretrained cross-modality feature matcher. Matched keypoints are back-projected to obtain 2D--3D correspondences for PnP-based pose estimation. Relying exclusively on geometry makes the method inherently robust to lighting and texture variation, while correspondence filtering handles geometric deviations between the model and physical object. We evaluate on widely-used public benchmarks, reporting state-of-the-art results on texture-less objects without object-specific training, and introduce a novel dataset with assembly defects, texture variations, and occlusion to demonstrate real-world applicability.

</details>


### [46] [Modularized Dynamic-Granularity Video LLM for Multi-Event Long Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15778)
*Wei Feng,Xin Wang,Yu-Wei Zhan,Yuwei Zhou,Wenwu Zhu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MoD-VLLM introduces a modular dynamic-granularity framework for long-video understanding that iteratively grounds question-relevant segments and selectively encodes positive and negative segments, achieving state-of-the-art results on long-video benchmarks including MEventBench.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Long videos pose limited visual token budgets while requiring detection of multiple key events; existing two-stage approaches lack adaptive capacity allocation and self-correction, leading to unreliable modeling.

Method: Propose MoD-VLLM with two modules: Positive-Negative Video Segments Grounding (PNGS) and Modularized Dynamic-Granularity Reflection (MDGR). Closed-loop grounding to localize relevant segments; grounding instructs Video LLM to separate relevant vs irrelevant segments; reflection module uses a modularized scheduler to apply fine-grained encoding to positive segments for detail and coarse-grained encoding to negative segments for global context; dynamic-granularity reinforcement learning to jointly learn grounding policies and granular representations; introduces MEventBench benchmark.

Result: Empirical results on several long-video benchmarks and MEventBench show significant improvements over state-of-the-art baselines.

Conclusion: The modular dynamic-granularity loop with adaptive grounding and reflection improves long-video reasoning; MEventBench provides a challenging testbed to evaluate multi-event long-video understanding.

Abstract: Video Large Language Models (Video LLMs) have made significant advancements in various video understanding tasks. However, long-video scenarios remain challenging due to the tension between limited visual token budgets and the need to capture multiple key events. Existing approaches typically process long videos in two stages, i.e., i) select keyframes and ii) perform detailed perception, which exhibit limitations: they lack a modular mechanism for adaptive capacity allocation and self-correction, resulting in unreliable modeling. To tackle these challenges, we propose MoD-VLLM, a novel Modularized Dynamic-Granularity Video LLM framework for multi-event long video understanding, which unifies temporal grounding and semantic understanding iteratively and self-reflectively. Specifically, we propose a Positive-Negative Video Segments Grounding module and a Modularized Dynamic-Granularity Reflection module, which form a closed loop to progressively localize the question-related video segments. The grounding module instructs a Video LLM to distinguish relevant from irrelevant video segments based on the video question. The reflection module employs a modularized scheduler that dynamically selects fine-grained encoding for relevant positive segments to capture detailed perception and coarse-grained encoding for negative segments to maintain global context. We further propose a dynamic-granularity reinforcement learning strategy, allowing MoD-VLLM to learn optimal grounding policies and dynamic granularity visual representation jointly. Moreover, we propose MEventBench, a challenging Multi-Event Long Video Benchmark for complex long video reasoning. Extensive experiments on several long video understanding benchmarks and our MEventBench demonstrate that MoD-VLLM significantly outperforms state-of-the-art baselines.

</details>


### [47] [Multimodal Ambivalence and Hesitancy Recognition via Cross-Attention and Gated Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15779)
*Oussama Berhili,Yassine Ouzar,Larbi Boubchir*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Multimodal fusion improves Ambivalence/Hesitancy recognition in video by combining textual, acoustic, and visual cues; unimodal baselines with text perform best, and cross-modal attention with a GMU yields the best validation Macro F1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address Ambivalence/Hesitancy recognition in video (A/H) by exploiting complementary information across text, audio, and visual cues on the BAH dataset within the ABAW11 ECCV 2026 challenge.

Method: Establish unimodal baselines using three encoders (F2LLM-v2-0.6B for transcripts, WavLM-Large for audio, VideoMAE V2 for facial video) with classical classifiers (MLP, Random Forest, GBDT) tuned via Optuna. Develop a multimodal fusion architecture with bidirectional cross-attention across the three modalities and a GMU, with hyperparameters tuned via a 50-trial Optuna search.

Result: Best unimodal Macro F1 on test using text alone: 0.6659 (outperforms zero-shot Video-LLaVA baseline: 0.2827). Multimodal fusion yields Macro F1 on validation: 0.7394, an 11.0% relative improvement over the best unimodal baseline.

Conclusion: Explicit cross-modal interaction captures complementary cues not available in a single modality, improving A/H recognition performance. The approach generalizes to private test data, with code released publicly.

Abstract: We present a multimodal framework for Ambivalence/Hesitancy (A/H) recognition in video, developed for the ABAW11 challenge at ECCV 2026. The proposed approach fuses textual, acoustic, and visual modalities extracted from the BAH dataset using three pretrained encoders: F2LLM-v2-0.6B for transcripts (1024-d), WavLM-Large for audio (1024-d), and VideoMAE V2 for facial video (768-d). We first establish comprehensive unimodal baselines using classical classifiers (MLP, Random Forest, GBDT), each optimized via Optuna, and obtain a best unimodal Macro F1 of \textbf{0.6659} on the test set using text features alone -- substantially outperforming the zero-shot Video-LLaVA baseline (Macro F1: 0.2827). Building on these baselines, we propose a multimodal fusion architecture that combines bidirectional cross-attention across all three modalities with a Gated Multimodal Unit (GMU), with both architectural and optimization hyperparameters selected through a 50-trial Optuna search. This model achieves a Macro F1 of \textbf{0.7394} on the validation set, a relative improvement of 11.0\% over the best unimodal baseline, confirming that explicit cross-modal interaction captures complementary cues that no single modality provides in isolation. Final predictions on the official, unlabeled private test set are generated using this model and submitted according to the challenge protocol. Code is publicly available at https://github.com/yassineouzar/IUSD_AH/

</details>


### [48] [On the Geometry of Learned Representations in Event-Based Multi-Modal Egomotion Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15794)
*Stefano Silvestrini,Michele Ceresoli*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The abstract analyzes the latent geometry of a cross-modal network for egomotion estimation using event tensors, inertials, and range data. It shows embeddings occupy low-dimensional motion-aligned manifolds, attention adapts to angular excitation and visual reliability, and the fused representation recovers classical observability cues, bridging analytical estimation theory with data-driven fusion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Explain how multi-modal fusion for egomotion estimation organizes latent space and attention, and how this relates to classical estimation concepts.

Method: Multi-modal network with cross-modal attention fusing event tensors, inertial measurements, and range signals; batch training; analysis of latent space geometry and attention dynamics; evaluation of observability cues.

Result: (i) Embeddings lie on low-dimensional manifolds aligned with motion variables; (ii) attention weights adapt with angular excitation and visual reliability; (iii) fused representation recovers classical observability cues.

Conclusion: Bridges analytical estimation theory and data-driven fusion, offering insights into latent geometry and attention behavior that align with classical observability concepts.

Abstract: Classical approaches to event-based egomotion estimation, including those adopted by the top-performing teams of the ELOPE challenge, rely on geometric optimization frameworks such as contrast maximization, homography estimation, or dense optical flow combined with analytic motion inversion. This work investigates the geometric structure that emerges inside a multi-modal network for egomotion estimation. Event tensors, inertial measurements, and range signals are fused through a cross-modal attention architecture and trained in a batch setting. We analyze the latent space geometry and attention dynamics, showing that (i) embeddings lie on low-dimensional manifolds aligned with motion variables, (ii) attention weights adapt with angular excitation and visual reliability, and (iii) the fused representation recovers classical observability cues. These results bridge analytical estimation theory and modern data-driven fusion.

</details>


### [49] [HybridSim: A Physics-Learning Hybrid Digital Twin for mmWave Human Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15806)
*Weitao Xiong,Tianyu Liu,Peng Li,Kok Chung Chua,Toa Chean Khim,Pu Wang,Hongfei Xue*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: HybridSim is a physics-learning hybrid simulator for mmWave radar signals from dynamic human meshes in a fixed indoor room, decoupling propagation into direct and indirect paths. It uses a tri-plane human representation and a Graph ConvNet for stable optimization, inverse-rendering with a microfacet BRDF for the direct path, and 3D Gaussian splatting with a virtual-receiver geometry for the indirect path to capture multipath at lower cost than full ray tracing. It yields improved physics agreement and downstream gains for site-specific data augmentation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-fidelity mmWave radar simulation for dynamic human motion is valuable but collecting accurately labeled measurements for deployment sites is expensive. A site-specific, computationally efficient simulator that decouples propagation components can enable effective data augmentation for radar-based sensing.

Method: Model a dynamic human subject with a tri-plane representation; optimize with a Graph Convolutional Network to stabilize gradients. Two propagation components: direct path via inverse-rendering with a microfacet BRDF; indirect path approximated by 3D Gaussian Splatting plus a virtual-receiver geometry to fit multipath in a fixed room. Operates in a fixed indoor configuration to reduce complexity.

Result: HybridSim achieves improved agreement with a physically based reference and provides consistent gains on downstream radar-based human sensing tasks when used for site-specific data augmentation, with substantially lower computational cost than explicit full ray tracing.

Conclusion: A physics-learning hybrid simulator can produce high-fidelity, site-specific mmWave radar signals in fixed environments by decoupling propagation, using learning-based optimization to stabilize the human model and efficient indirect-path modeling to capture multipath, enabling effective data augmentation with lower computational cost.

Abstract: High-fidelity simulation of mmWave radar signals for dynamic human motion is valuable for developing radar-based human sensing models; yet collecting accurately labeled measurements for a specific deployment site remains expensive. We present HybridSim, a physics-learning hybrid simulator that synthesizes mmWave radar signals from dynamic human meshes under a fixed indoor room configuration, explicitly decoupling propagation into two components. To parameterize the human subject, we use a tri-plane representation to extract human features and a Graph Convolutional Network to stabilize optimization and mitigate gradient instability. The direct signal path is modeled via an inverse-rendering formulation with a microfacet BRDF to capture primary surface reflections. In parallel, the indirect path is approximated by combining 3D Gaussian Splatting with a virtual-receiver geometry to fit and reproduce site-specific multipath interference patterns, achieving substantially lower computational cost than explicit full ray tracing. Experiments in a fixed-room setting show improved agreement with a physically based reference and consistent gains on downstream radar-based human sensing tasks when using HybridSim for site-specific data augmentation.

</details>


### [50] [Examining the Associations between Visual and Non-Visual Elements and Cyclists' Route Choices for Various Trip Purposes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15808)
*Heyang Hua,Koichi Ito,Filip Biljecki*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A data-driven Montreal study investigates how visual (street-view) and non-visual (socioeconomic and 2D environmental) factors shape cyclists' route choices for different trip purposes, using spatiotemporal analysis and shortest-vs-actual-path comparisons to derive design implications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Clarify the combined influence of visual perception and non-visual environmental factors on cycling route choices beyond simple shortest-distance optimization, addressing trip-purpose heterogeneity.

Method: Two-part, data-driven Montreal case study: (1) spatiotemporal analysis of start-end points to quantify non-visual factors (socioeconomics and 2D environmental attributes); (2) compare distributions of these factors between the shortest-path and actual cycling routes, incorporating visual cues estimated from street-view images.

Result: Identifies spatiotemporal characteristics that influence cycling choices, notably higher greenery and lower motorization along chosen routes; demonstrates that visual-perception metrics from street views relate to route choice and that effects vary by trip purpose; provides actionable insights for street-network planning and active-mobility infrastructure.

Conclusion: Non-visual and visual environmental attributes jointly shape cyclists’ route decisions. The findings support planning that increases greenery and reduces motorized intrusions on cycling corridors and demonstrate a scalable, data-driven approach for informing urban design and policy to promote active transport.

Abstract: Understanding cyclist preferences for the characteristics of the built environment is important in promoting sustainable urban transportation and active mobility. Despite previous studies on cyclists' route choices, the influence of visual and non-visual factors on these choices for different trip purposes remains unclear; thus, this paper fills this gap through a data-driven case study in Montreal, Canada. Non-visual factors include socioeconomic factors and two-dimensional environments, while visual factors involve visual perception during cycling and are computed using street view images. The study consists of two parts: one part analyzes spatiotemporal information to explore the non-visual factors between the start and end points of cycling trips, and the other part investigates the discrepancies in distributions of these factors between the shortest path and the actual one. The findings reveal the spatiotemporal characteristics that influence active riding choices, such as increased greenery and lower levels of motorization. These insights can inform the planning of street networks and the development of infrastructure to improve the use of active transportation.

</details>


### [51] [Test-Time Noise Guided Adaptation for Realistic Autoregressive Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15849)
*Dimitrios Karageorgiou,Symeon Papadopoulos,Ioannis Kompatsiaris,Efstratios Gavves*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TANGO: a test-time prior to avoid terminal points in autoregressive video diffusion by using the model as a critic to predict a forward step and enforce a Gaussian noise distribution; yields improvements on VBench and FVD with released code.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Addresses error accumulation and terminal-point traps in autoregressive video diffusion, where frame-wise manifold anchoring helps but can still lead to trajectories exiting the learned manifold. Seeks a test-time prior that biases trajectories toward plausible future noise patterns.

Method: Introduce Terminal points Avoidance through Noise Guided Optimization (TANGO). At test time, the diffusion model predicts one step forward and requires the noise to be consistent with an isotropic Gaussian. The deviation from this expected noise distribution guides an optimization to select an alternative trajectory that avoids terminal points. The diffusion model acts as a critic of its own outputs, steering generation away from dead-ends.

Result: Achieves 3.1 percentage point absolute improvement on VBench over state-of-the-art baselines and reduces Fréchet Video Distance (FVD) by 28.3% on average for 15-second videos. Code is released at the given URL.

Conclusion: TANGO provides a practical, model-guided test-time prior to maintain plausible future trajectories in autoregressive video diffusion, improving long-horizon generation quality and stability with notable gains on standard metrics.

Abstract: Autoregressive video diffusion models have enabled the generation of arbitrarily long videos by removing conditioning on future frames, thus greatly improving computational efficiency. Yet, they suffer from error accumulation over time, as the denoised sequence gradually drifts away from the conditioning distribution seen during training. Recent advances attempt to reduce this error by anchoring each generated frame to the learned manifold of real ones. However, even when all generated individual frames lie close to the real manifold, there are trajectories which the model lacks sufficient knowledge to continue without exiting it, thus reaching a terminal point. To prevent the model from being trapped in terminal points, we start from the hypothesis that for well-modeled future trajectories the distribution of the predicted noise should match the one of the forward noising process. To enforce such a prior at test time, we introduce Terminal points Avoidance through Noise Guided Optimization (TANGO), which uses the diffusion model as a critic of its own outputs, by predicting one step forward and requiring an isotropic Gaussian noise prediction.
